📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Convert to 24-hour clock time: (a) 6:15 am, (b) 2:45 pm, (c) 11:20 pm, (d) 12:00 midnight. (e) If a train departs at 08:45 and arrives at 14:15, what is the journey duration? Time Conversion & Calculation 4 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Part 1: Conversions to 24-Hour Time:
• (a) 6:15 am = 06:15 hours (am times before noon remain unchanged).
• (b) 2:45 pm = 2:45 + 12:00 = 14:45 hours.
• (c) 11:20 pm = 11:20 + 12:00 = 23:20 hours.
• (d) 12:00 midnight = 00:00 hours (or 24:00 hours).

Part 2: Train Journey Duration:
• Departure: 08:45 hours.
• From 08:45 to 09:00 = 15 minutes.
• From 09:00 to 14:00 = 5 hours.
• From 14:00 to 14:15 = 15 minutes.
• Total duration = 5 hours + 15 min + 15 min = 5 hours 30 minutes.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct conversions for all 4 time formats 2.0 M
Accurate step-by-step elapsed time calculation (5h 30m) 2.0 M
Q2 Convert the following times between 12-hour clock (am/pm) and 24-hour railway clock:
(a) 07:45 am
(b) 03:20 pm
(c) 21:50 hours Time Arithmetic • 24-Hour Railway Clock 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Time Conversions:
• (a) 07:45 am: Since it is am before noon, the time remains 07:45 hours.
• (b) 03:20 pm: Since it is pm after noon, add 12 hours: 3:20 + 12:00 = 15:20 hours.
• (c) 21:50 hours: Since it is greater than 12:00, subtract 12 hours: 21:50 - 12:00 = 09:50 pm.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
(a) 07:45 hours 1.0 M
(b) 15:20 hours 1.0 M
(c) 09:50 pm 1.0 M
Q3 A superfast train departs from New Delhi at 06:15 am and arrives in Amritsar on the same day at 01:45 pm. What is the total duration of the train journey? Elapsed Time • Journey Duration 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Elapsed Time Calculation:
• Departure Time: 06:15 am
• Arrival Time: 01:45 pm

1. From 06:15 am to 12:15 pm (noon) = 6 hours.
2. From 12:15 pm to 01:15 pm = 1 hour.
3. From 01:15 pm to 01:45 pm = 30 minutes.

Total Journey Time = 6 hours + 1 hour + 30 minutes = 7 hours 30 minutes.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Decomposing time to noon milestone 1.5 M
Accurate sum: 7 hours 30 minutes 1.5 M
Q4 A packet of butter shows 'Manufacture Date: 15/04/2026' and 'Best before 180 days from packaging'. Can it be safely consumed on 15th October 2026? Show calculations. Calendar Literacy • Expiry Dates 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Days Count Calculation:
• Days remaining in April (after 15th): 30 - 15 = 15 days.
• May: 31 days.
• June: 30 days.
• July: 31 days.
• August: 31 days.
• September: 30 days.
• Total days elapsed by Sept 30 = 15 + 31 + 30 + 31 + 31 + 30 = 168 days.

Remaining valid days for October = 180 - 168 = 12 days.
Hence, the product expires on 12th October 2026.
Therefore, on 15th October 2026 (183 days), the butter is expired and unsafe to consume.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Accurate month-by-month day counting (168 days up to Sept 30) 1.5 M
Calculating expiry on Oct 12 and concluding unsafe on Oct 15 1.5 M
Q5 In Chapter 12 ('Ticking Clocks and Turning Calendar'), a classroom project investigates real-world mathematics: If a school bus travels 48 kilometres on 4 litres of diesel, what is the mileage (kilometres per litre) of the bus? How much diesel is needed for a 120-kilometre journey? Textbook Exercise • Real-World Context 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
1. Mileage calculation:
• Distance = 48 km, Diesel used = 4 L.
• Mileage = 48 ÷ 4 = 12 km per litre.
2. Diesel for 120 km:
• Required diesel = Total distance ÷ Mileage = 120 ÷ 12 = 10 litres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Mileage = 12 km/L 1.5 M
Diesel needed = 10 L 1.5 M
Q6 Convert the following measurements into standard units:
(a) 4 metres 65 centimetres into centimetres.
(b) 3500 millilitres into litres and millilitres.
(c) 2 kilograms 750 grams into grams. Practice Time • Measurement & Conversions 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Conversions:
• (a) 1 m = 100 cm → (4 × 100) + 65 = 465 cm.
• (b) 1000 mL = 1 L → 3500 mL = 3 L 500 mL.
• (c) 1 kg = 1000 g → (2 × 1000) + 750 = 2750 g.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
465 cm 1 M
3 L 500 mL 1 M
2750 g 1 M
Q7 Look at a solid brick:
(a) How many faces does a brick have?
(b) What is the shape of each face?
(c) How many straight edges and how many corners (vertices) does a brick have? Try These • Geometry and Shapes 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Properties of a Brick (Cuboid):
• (a) Faces: A brick has 6 flat faces.
• (b) Shape of faces: Each face is a rectangle.
• (c) Edges and Corners: It has 12 straight edges and 8 corners (vertices).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
6 faces 1 M
Rectangular faces 1 M
12 edges and 8 corners 1 M
Q8 At a recycling shop, the rate list shows: Waste paper = ₹12 per kg, Plastic = ₹18 per kg, Iron = ₹25 per kg.
Ramesh sells 10 kg of waste paper, 5 kg of plastic, and 4 kg of iron.
(a) Find the cost of each item sold.
(b) What is the total money Ramesh earns? Money & Accounts • Rate List Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Calculations:
• Waste paper: 10 × ₹12 = ₹120.
• Plastic: 5 × ₹18 = ₹90.
• Iron: 4 × ₹25 = ₹100.
• Total Earnings: ₹120 + ₹90 + ₹100 = ₹310.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Individual costs (₹120, ₹90, ₹100) 1.5 M
Total = ₹310 1.5 M
Q9 A rectangular chocolate bar has 16 equal pieces. Reena gives 1/4 of the chocolate to her brother and 1/2 of it to her friend.
(a) How many pieces does her brother receive?
(b) How many pieces does her friend receive?
(c) How many pieces are left for Reena? Fractions Exploration • Parts of a Whole 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Breakdown:
• Total pieces = 16.
• Brother's share (1/4): 16 ÷ 4 = 4 pieces.
• Friend's share (1/2): 16 ÷ 2 = 8 pieces.
• Remaining for Reena: 16 - (4 + 8) = 16 - 12 = 4 pieces (which is 1/4 of the bar).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Brother = 4 pieces 1 M
Friend = 8 pieces 1 M
Reena = 4 pieces 1 M
Q10 A school cricket match began at 9:30 AM and concluded at 1:15 PM.
(a) Convert 1:15 PM into 24-hour railway clock time.
(b) How long did the cricket match last? Time & Clocks • Elapsed Time 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Time Calculations:
• (a) 24-hour time: 1:15 PM = 12:00 + 1:15 = 13:15 hours.
• (b) Duration:
• From 9:30 AM to 12:30 PM = 3 hours.
• From 12:30 PM to 1:15 PM = 45 minutes.
• Total duration = 3 hours and 45 minutes.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
13:15 hours in 24-hr clock 1 M
Duration = 3 hours 45 minutes with steps 2 M
Q11 A square vegetable garden has a side length of 15 metres.
(a) What is the total length of wire fence needed to go around the garden once?
(b) If fencing costs ₹20 per metre, find the total cost of fencing. Perimeter & Area • Fencing a Field 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Perimeter & Cost:
• (a) Perimeter of square: 4 × Side = 4 × 15 m = 60 metres.
• (b) Total Cost: Perimeter × Rate = 60 m × ₹20/m = ₹1,200.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Perimeter = 60 m 1.5 M
Cost = ₹1,200 1.5 M
Q12 Solve mentally using number breakdown:
(a) 72 ÷ 6 = ?
(b) 15 × 8 = ?
(c) 25 × 40 = ?
(d) 96 ÷ 8 = ? Mental Math • Division and Multiplication 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Solutions:
• (a) 72 ÷ 6 = (60 ÷ 6) + (12 ÷ 6) = 10 + 2 = 12.
• (b) 15 × 8 = (10 × 8) + (5 × 8) = 80 + 40 = 120.
• (c) 25 × 40 = (25 × 4) × 10 = 100 × 10 = 1000.
• (d) 96 ÷ 8 = (80 ÷ 8) + (16 ÷ 8) = 10 + 2 = 12.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
All 4 mental math facts correct 2 M
Q13 Look at the letter 'H' and the letter 'N':
(a) What does each look like after a half-turn (180° rotation)?
(b) What does each look like after a quarter-turn (90° rotation)? Patterns & Symmetries • Rotational Turns 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Rotational Symmetry:
• (a) Half-turn (180°): Both 'H' and 'N' look exactly the same after a half-turn.
• (b) Quarter-turn (90°): 'H' rotates to look like 'I' with sleeping bars, and 'N' rotates to look like 'Z'.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Half-turn explanation (same) 1 M
Quarter-turn explanation 1 M
Q14 In a Class 4 survey of 40 children, 20 like Cricket, 10 like Football, and 10 like Badminton.
(a) What fraction of children like Cricket?
(b) Draw a simple circular pie chart (Chapati Chart) representing this data. Smart Charts • Data Interpretation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Data & Chapati Chart:
• (a) Fraction: 20 out of 40 = 20/40 = 1/2 (half of the class).
• (b) Chapati Chart: A circle divided in half represents Cricket (1/2). The other half is split equally into two quarters (1/4 for Football and 1/4 for Badminton).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Fraction = 1/2 for cricket 1.5 M
Pie chart sectors explained (1/2, 1/4, 1/4) 1.5 M
Q15 State True or False:
(a) A circle has no corners and no straight edges.
(b) 1 litre is equal to 100 millilitres.
(c) The perimeter of any rectangle is 2 × (Length + Breadth). Chapter Review • True or False 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Answers:
• (a) True: A circle is a smooth curve with 0 corners.
• (b) False: 1 Litre is equal to 1000 millilitres.
• (c) True: Perimeter = 2 × (L + B).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
True, False, True with explanations 3 M
🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Medicine Expiry Date Verification

Real-World Math Context: A medicine bottle has manufacturing date 'MFG: 03/24' and states 'Best before 24 months from manufacture'.

Analytical Task: Can this medicine be safely consumed in February 2026? What is the last safe month?

Analysis:
• Manufacturing date = March 2024 (03/24).
• Adding 24 months (2 full years): March 2024 + 2 years = March 2026.
Conclusion:
Yes, it can be safely consumed in February 2026 because February 2026 falls before the expiry date of March 2026.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for calculating expiry month (March 2026); 1 Mark for valid consumption conclusion.
13
Unit 5: Financial Literacy, Fares & Travel Planning • Class 4 Mathematics (New NEP 2020)

Chapter 13: The Transport Museum

Ticket Booking, Travel Fares, Currency Operations (Rupees & Paise) & Timetables

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Financial arithmetic involving Indian Currency: Rupees (₹) and Paise (p); 1 Rupee = 100 Paise.
  • Reading and interpreting bus/train fare charts and ticket rates for adults and children.
  • Calculating change returned by cashiers during purchases.
  • Planning trip budgets and comparing transport choices.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 A family of 2 adults and 3 children visits the Transport Museum. Ticket rates are ₹85 per adult and ₹40 per child. (a) What is the total ticket cost? (b) The father pays with a ₹500 currency note. How much change does the booking clerk return? Currency Operations & Change Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Total Ticket Cost:
• Cost for 2 adults = 2 × ₹85 = ₹170.
• Cost for 3 children = 3 × ₹40 = ₹120.
• Total cost = ₹170 + ₹120 = ₹290.

(b) Change Returned:
• Amount given = ₹500.
• Total bill = ₹290.
• Change = ₹500 - ₹290 = ₹210.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct adult and child ticket total (₹290) 1.5 M
Correct change calculation (₹210) 1.5 M
Q2 At the transport museum gift shop, Kabir buys 3 toy vintage cars at ₹125 each, 2 model aeroplanes at ₹240 each, and 4 souvenir keychains at ₹35 each. Prepare a cash memo and calculate the total bill. Financial Literacy • Creating a Cash Bill 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Cash Memo / Bill Preparation:
-----------------------------------------------------------
Item                Quantity    Rate (₹)      Amount (₹)
-----------------------------------------------------------
1. Toy Vintage Cars      3          ₹125          ₹375
2. Model Aeroplanes      2          ₹240          ₹480
3. Souvenir Keychains    4           ₹35          ₹140
-----------------------------------------------------------
Total Bill Amount = ₹375 + ₹480 + ₹140 = ₹995.
If Kabir pays with a ₹1,000 note, change returned = ₹1,000 - ₹995 = ₹5.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct subtotal for all three items 1.5 M
Correct grand total of ₹995 and change ₹5 1.5 M
Q3 If 8 bus tickets from the city centre to the museum cost ₹360, how much will 15 tickets cost? Unitary Method • Real-World Math 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Unitary Method Solution:
1. Cost of 1 ticket:
₹360 ÷ 8 = ₹45 per ticket.

2. Cost of 15 tickets:
15 × ₹45 = ₹675.

The cost of 15 bus tickets is ₹675.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Unit cost calculation: ₹360 ÷ 8 = ₹45 1.0 M
Final cost calculation: 15 × ₹45 = ₹675 1.0 M
Q4 How many ₹50 notes make ₹2,500? How many ₹200 notes make ₹1,600? Money Arithmetic • Currency Denominations 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Currency Calculations:
• (a) ₹2,500 ÷ ₹50 = 50 notes of ₹50.
• (b) ₹1,600 ÷ ₹200 = 8 notes of ₹200.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
(a) 50 notes 1.0 M
(b) 8 notes 1.0 M

Q12 Solve mentally using number breakdown:

(c) 25 × 40 = ?
(d) 96 ÷ 8 = ? Mental Math • Division and Multiplication 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Solutions:

• (b) 15 × 8 = (10 × 8) + (5 × 8) = 80 + 40 = 120.
• (c) 25 × 40 = (25 × 4) × 10 = 100 × 10 = 1000.
• (d) 96 ÷ 8 = (80 ÷ 8) + (16 ÷ 8) = 10 + 2 = 12.

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Toy Train Ride Booking

Real-World Math Context: The museum toy train operates every 20 minutes from 10:00 am. It accommodates 32 passengers per trip.

Analytical Task: How many trips will the train make between 10:00 am and 12:00 noon, and what is the maximum number of visitors it can carry?

Trip Calculation:
• Duration from 10:00 am to 12:00 noon = 2 hours = 120 minutes.
• Number of trips = 120 min ÷ 20 min = 6 trips (at 10:00, 10:20, 10:40, 11:00, 11:20, 11:40).
• Maximum visitors = 6 trips × 32 passengers = 192 passengers.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for 6 trips; 1 Mark for 192 total passengers.

Unit 5: Data Representation & Interpretation • Class 4 Mathematics (New NEP 2020)

Chapter 14: Data Handling

Pictographs, Bar Graphs, Tally Tables & Frequency Distribution

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Representing information using pictographs with a defined scale key (e.g., 1 icon = 5 students).
  • Drawing and reading vertical and horizontal bar graphs with labeled axes.
  • Finding maximum, minimum, and difference values from graphical data.
  • Drawing valid conclusions and inferences from statistical representations.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 In a pictograph, the symbol 📚 represents 6 library books borrowed. Look at the data: Class 4A has 5 symbols; Class 4B has 4 symbols; Class 4C has 6 symbols; Class 4D has 3 and a half symbols. (a) How many books were borrowed by each class? (b) What is the total number of books borrowed? Pictograph & Scale Interpretation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Scale: 1 symbol 📚 = 6 books.
Half symbol = 6 ÷ 2 = 3 books.

(a) Books borrowed by each section:
• Class 4A: 5 × 6 = 30 books.
• Class 4B: 4 × 6 = 24 books.
• Class 4C: 6 × 6 = 36 books.
• Class 4D: (3 × 6) + 3 = 18 + 3 = 21 books.

(b) Total books borrowed:
Total = 30 + 24 + 36 + 21 = 111 books.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct calculation for all 4 sections with half-symbol logic 2.0 M
Accurate sum of 111 books 1.0 M
Q2 In a school library pictograph, one symbol 📖 represents 10 books. The records show:
• Science: 📖 📖 📖 📖
• Maths: 📖 📖 📖 📖 📖 📖
• Storybooks: 📖 📖 📖 📖 📖 📖 📖 📖
(a) How many Maths books are there? (b) How many more Storybooks are there than Science books? Data Handling • Pictograph Interpretation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Pictograph Reading (Key: 📖 = 10 books):
• Science = 4 symbols × 10 = 40 books.
• Maths = 6 symbols × 10 = 60 books.
• Storybooks = 8 symbols × 10 = 80 books.

1. (a) Number of Maths books: 60 books.
2. (b) Difference between Storybooks and Science:
80 - 40 = 40 more Storybooks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Using key scale of 10 books accurately 1.0 M
Maths books count = 60 1.0 M
Difference = 40 books 1.0 M
Q3 A survey of 40 children about their favourite sport revealed: 20 like Cricket, 10 like Football, and 10 like Badminton. Represent this data as fractions on a circular Chapati Chart. Data Visualization • Chapati (Pie) Chart 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Circular Chart Representation (Total = 40 children):
• Cricket: 20 out of 40 = 20/40 = 1/2 (Half of the circle).
• Football: 10 out of 40 = 10/40 = 1/4 (One-fourth / quarter of the circle).
• Badminton: 10 out of 40 = 10/40 = 1/4 (One-fourth / quarter of the circle).

On the circular chart, draw a straight diameter dividing the circle into two halves; label one half 'Cricket (1/2)'. Divide the other half into two equal quarters; label one 'Football (1/4)' and the other 'Badminton (1/4)'.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Calculating correct fractional share (1/2, 1/4, 1/4) 1.5 M
Describing accurate geometric sector partition of circle 1.5 M
Q4 Represent the number 18 using standard 5-bar tally marks. Tally Marks • Organizing Raw Data 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Tally Marks for 18:
Standard tally marks group in clusters of five (four vertical bars crossed by a diagonal fifth bar ||||):
• 18 = 5 + 5 + 5 + 3
• ||||   ||||   ||||   |||
(Three groups of five plus three individual tally bars).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Three groups of 5 tallies plus 3 singles correctly written 2.0 M
Q5 In Chapter 14 ('Data Handling'), a classroom project investigates real-world mathematics: If a school bus travels 48 kilometres on 4 litres of diesel, what is the mileage (kilometres per litre) of the bus? How much diesel is needed for a 120-kilometre journey? Textbook Exercise • Real-World Context 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
1. Mileage calculation:
• Distance = 48 km, Diesel used = 4 L.
• Mileage = 48 ÷ 4 = 12 km per litre.
2. Diesel for 120 km:
• Required diesel = Total distance ÷ Mileage = 120 ÷ 12 = 10 litres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Mileage = 12 km/L 1.5 M
Diesel needed = 10 L 1.5 M
Q6 Convert the following measurements into standard units:
(a) 4 metres 65 centimetres into centimetres.
(b) 3500 millilitres into litres and millilitres.
(c) 2 kilograms 750 grams into grams. Practice Time • Measurement & Conversions 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Conversions:
• (a) 1 m = 100 cm → (4 × 100) + 65 = 465 cm.
• (b) 1000 mL = 1 L → 3500 mL = 3 L 500 mL.
• (c) 1 kg = 1000 g → (2 × 1000) + 750 = 2750 g.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
465 cm 1 M
3 L 500 mL 1 M
2750 g 1 M
Q7 Look at a solid brick:
(a) How many faces does a brick have?
(b) What is the shape of each face?
(c) How many straight edges and how many corners (vertices) does a brick have? Try These • Geometry and Shapes 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Properties of a Brick (Cuboid):
• (a) Faces: A brick has 6 flat faces.
• (b) Shape of faces: Each face is a rectangle.
• (c) Edges and Corners: It has 12 straight edges and 8 corners (vertices).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
6 faces 1 M
Rectangular faces 1 M
12 edges and 8 corners 1 M
Q8 At a recycling shop, the rate list shows: Waste paper = ₹12 per kg, Plastic = ₹18 per kg, Iron = ₹25 per kg.
Ramesh sells 10 kg of waste paper, 5 kg of plastic, and 4 kg of iron.
(a) Find the cost of each item sold.
(b) What is the total money Ramesh earns? Money & Accounts • Rate List Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Calculations:
• Waste paper: 10 × ₹12 = ₹120.
• Plastic: 5 × ₹18 = ₹90.
• Iron: 4 × ₹25 = ₹100.
• Total Earnings: ₹120 + ₹90 + ₹100 = ₹310.

Q9 A rectangular chocolate bar has 16 equal pieces. Reena gives 1/4 of the chocolate to her brother and 1/2 of it to her friend.
(a) How many pieces does her brother receive?
(b) How many pieces does her friend receive?
(c) How many pieces are left for Reena? Fractions Exploration • Parts of a Whole 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Breakdown:
• Total pieces = 16.
• Brother's share (1/4): 16 ÷ 4 = 4 pieces.
• Friend's share (1/2): 16 ÷ 2 = 8 pieces.
• Remaining for Reena: 16 - (4 + 8) = 16 - 12 = 4 pieces (which is 1/4 of the bar).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Brother = 4 pieces 1 M
Friend = 8 pieces 1 M
Reena = 4 pieces 1 M
Q10 A school cricket match began at 9:30 AM and concluded at 1:15 PM.

✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Time Calculations:
• (a) 24-hour time: 1:15 PM = 12:00 + 1:15 = 13:15 hours.
• (b) Duration:
• From 9:30 AM to 12:30 PM = 3 hours.
• From 12:30 PM to 1:15 PM = 45 minutes.
• Total duration = 3 hours and 45 minutes.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
13:15 hours in 24-hr clock 1 M
Duration = 3 hours 45 minutes with steps 2 M
Q11 A square vegetable garden has a side length of 15 metres.

• (b) Total Cost: Perimeter × Rate = 60 m × ₹20/m = ₹1,200.

Q12 Solve mentally using number breakdown:
(a) 72 ÷ 6 = ?
(b) 15 × 8 = ?
(c) 25 × 40 = ?
(d) 96 ÷ 8 = ? Mental Math • Division and Multiplication 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Solutions:
• (a) 72 ÷ 6 = (60 ÷ 6) + (12 ÷ 6) = 10 + 2 = 12.
• (b) 15 × 8 = (10 × 8) + (5 × 8) = 80 + 40 = 120.
• (c) 25 × 40 = (25 × 4) × 10 = 100 × 10 = 1000.
• (d) 96 ÷ 8 = (80 ÷ 8) + (16 ÷ 8) = 10 + 2 = 12.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
All 4 mental math facts correct 2 M
Q13 Look at the letter 'H' and the letter 'N':

✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Rotational Symmetry:
• (a) Half-turn (180°): Both 'H' and 'N' look exactly the same after a half-turn.
• (b) Quarter-turn (90°): 'H' rotates to look like 'I' with sleeping bars, and 'N' rotates to look like 'Z'.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Half-turn explanation (same) 1 M
Quarter-turn explanation 1 M
Q14 In a Class 4 survey of 40 children, 20 like Cricket, 10 like Football, and 10 like Badminton.

• (b) Chapati Chart: A circle divided in half represents Cricket (1/2). The other half is split equally into two quarters (1/4 for Football and 1/4 for Badminton).

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

School Canteen Drink Preferences

Real-World Math Context: A bar graph shows fruit juice sales: Lemonade = 45 glasses, Apple Juice = 30 glasses, Orange Juice = 55 glasses, and Coconut Water = 70 glasses.

Analytical Task: Which drink is the most popular, and how many more glasses of coconut water were sold than apple juice?

Analysis:
• Most popular drink = Coconut Water (70 glasses).
• Difference between coconut water and apple juice = 70 - 30 = 40 glasses.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for identifying coconut water; 1 Mark for 40 glasses difference.
1
Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 1: Building with Bricks

Brick Patterns, Arch Designs (Mehrab, Jharokha), Brick Geometry & Brick Kiln Arithmetic

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Architectural brick patterns from Jagriti School, Murshidabad: floor patterns, circular patterns, and mirror patterns.
  • Geometric dimensions and faces of a standard brick: 6 rectangular faces, 12 edges, 8 corners.
  • Traditional Indian architectural arches: Mehrab, circular arches, and decorative Jharokhas.
  • Large number calculations: Indian brick kilns producing 1,00,000 (one lakh) bricks; calculating the cost of thousands of bricks.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Answer the following questions about a brick: (a) How many faces does a brick have? (b) Is any face of a standard brick a square? (c) Draw the smallest face of a brick. (d) Which of the following shapes can be a face of a brick: Triangle, Square, Rectangle, Circle? Textbook Exercise • Geometric Properties of a Brick 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) A standard brick has 6 rectangular faces.
(b) No, in standard construction bricks, all 6 faces are rectangles of varying dimensions (length × width, length × height, width × height). None of the faces is a square.
(c) Smallest face: The side face measuring width × height (approximately 10 cm × 7 cm) is the smallest face.
(d) Face shape: Only a Rectangle can be a face of a standard brick (triangles and circles cannot form brick faces).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct face count (6) and shape verification 1.5 M
Smallest face description and rectangle identification 1.5 M
Q2 Bhajan went to buy bricks. The rate was ₹1,800 for 1,000 old bricks, ₹2,000 for 1,000 new bricks from Brickabad, and ₹1,200 for 1,000 bricks from Itapur. If Bhajan buys 3,000 new bricks from Brickabad and 500 old bricks, how much money will he pay in total? Brick Kiln Cost Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step 1: Cost of 3,000 new bricks from Brickabad:
• Cost of 1,000 new bricks = ₹2,000.
• Cost of 3,000 new bricks = 3 × ₹2,000 = ₹6,000.

Step 2: Cost of 500 old bricks:
• Cost of 1,000 old bricks = ₹1,800.
• Cost of 500 bricks (half of 1,000) = ₹1,800 ÷ 2 = ₹900.

Step 3: Total Payment:
• Total = ₹6,000 + ₹900 = ₹6,900.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Calculation for 3,000 new bricks (₹6,000) 1.0 M
Calculation for 500 old bricks (₹900) 1.0 M
Total payment ₹6,900 with clear working 1.0 M
Q3 What is an 'Arch' (कमान) in brick architecture? Where can you see traditional brick jali patterns in historical Indian monuments? Architectural Patterns • Arches & Jali 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
1. Brick Arch (Kaman):
An arch is a curved symmetrical structural opening built of wedge-shaped bricks arranged over an entrance or bridge. The bricks support each other by pressing against one another, transferring heavy downward weight safely to the side pillars without needing a horizontal wooden beam.

2. Traditional Brick Jali (Lattice):
Brick jalis are decorative screen walls created by leaving systematic hollow spaces between bricks during masonry. They allow cool breeze and soft geometric light patterns to enter while keeping harsh sun and rain out. Famous examples include the Jaagrithi School in Murshidabad (West Bengal) and historical Mughal monuments.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Structural principle of brick arch and weight distribution 1.5 M
Jali pattern description and historical monument examples 1.5 M
Q4 Bhajan went to buy bricks. The price of new bricks from Brickabad was ₹2,000 for 1,000 bricks. He bought 3,000 bricks. If he also bought 500 old bricks at ₹1,200 for 1,000 bricks, how much total money did he pay? Commercial Arithmetic • Cost of Bricks 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Calculation:
1. Cost of New Bricks from Brickabad:
• Cost of 1,000 bricks = ₹2,000.
• Cost of 3,000 bricks = 3 × ₹2,000 = ₹6,000.

2. Cost of Old Bricks:
• Cost of 1,000 bricks = ₹1,200.
• Cost of 500 bricks (half of 1,000) = ₹1,200 ÷ 2 = ₹600.

3. Total Money Paid:
₹6,000 + ₹600 = ₹6,600.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cost of new bricks (₹6,000) 1.0 M
Cost of old bricks (₹600) 1.0 M
Total sum: ₹6,600 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.
(a) What is the shape of a brick?
(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M

Q11 A cyclist has a bicycle wheel with a radius of 35 cm.

Q13 In a secret code, each letter is replaced by its position in the alphabet (A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26).

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Estimating Bricks for a Compound Wall

Real-World Math Context: A mason calculates that 1 metre length of a 1-metre high boundary wall requires 100 bricks. The school desires to construct a 45-metre long and 1-metre high wall.

Analytical Task: How many bricks should the school order? If each truck carries 2,500 bricks, how many truckloads are needed?

Step 1: Total bricks needed:
45 m × 100 bricks/m = 4,500 bricks.
Step 2: Number of truckloads:
4,500 ÷ 2,500 = 1 full truck (2,500 bricks) with 2,000 bricks remaining.
Therefore, 2 truckloads must be ordered so the project has enough bricks.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for 4,500 bricks; 1 Mark for practical answer of 2 truckloads.

Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 2: Long and Short

Measuring Heights, Distances, Conversions (cm, m, km), Sports Records & Marathon Math

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Comparing lengths visually and measuring accurately using a ruler and measuring tape.
  • Units of length: 1 m = 100 cm; 1 km = 1,000 m.
  • Analyzing world records and Indian records in high jump and long jump.
  • Calculating distances in round tracks and solving marathon running problems.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Look at the athletic records: Javier S. holds the Men's World Record for High Jump at 2 m 45 cm. Chandrapal holds the Indian Record for High Jump at 2 m 17 cm. (a) How many more centimetres must Chandrapal jump to equal Javier's world record? (b) How many centimetres short of 2 and a half metres is Javier's jump? Sports Records Comparison 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Difference between World and Indian High Jump records:
• Javier S. (World Record) = 2 m 45 cm = 245 cm.
• Chandrapal (Indian Record) = 2 m 17 cm = 217 cm.
• Difference = 245 cm - 217 cm = 28 cm.
Chandrapal must jump 28 cm higher.

(b) Short of 2 and a half metres (2 m 50 cm):
• 2 and a half metres = 2 m 50 cm = 250 cm.
• Javier's jump = 245 cm.
• Difference = 250 cm - 245 cm = 5 cm.
Javier's jump is only 5 cm short of 2.5 metres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct difference of 28 cm with conversion 1.5 M
Correct calculation showing 5 cm short of 2.5 m 1.5 M
Q2 A stadium running track is 400 metres long for one complete lap. People run a marathon race of 40 kilometres. How many complete rounds of the stadium track must a runner make to complete the 40 km marathon? Marathon & Stadium Track Math 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step 1: Convert marathon distance into metres:
• 1 kilometre = 1,000 metres.
• 40 km = 40 × 1,000 = 40,000 metres.

Step 2: Calculate number of rounds:
• Length of 1 round of stadium = 400 metres.
• Number of rounds = Total distance ÷ Track length
• Number of rounds = 40,000 ÷ 400 = 100 rounds.

A runner must complete exactly 100 rounds of the stadium track.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Unit conversion 40 km = 40,000 m 1.5 M
Division 40,000 ÷ 400 = 100 rounds 1.5 M
Q3 In high jump, Javier S. holds the world record of 2 m 45 cm, and Chandra Pal holds the Indian record of 2 m 17 cm. How many centimetres higher must Chandra Pal jump to equal the world record? Sports Arithmetic • High Jump & Long Jump 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Difference Calculation:
• World Record = 2 m 45 cm = 245 cm
• Indian Record = 2 m 17 cm = 217 cm

Difference = 245 cm - 217 cm = 28 cm.

Chandra Pal must jump 28 centimetres higher to equal the world record.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme

Q4 A doctor tells Devi Prasad to run 2 km every day to stay fit. He runs around a square field with each side 500 metres long. (a) How many rounds of this field must he take to complete 2 km? (b) If he instead runs on a 400-metre circular stadium track, how many rounds are needed? Marathon & Distance • Stadium Track 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution (Target = 2 km = 2,000 m):
1. (a) Square Field:
• Side = 500 m
• Perimeter of square = 4 × 500 m = 2,000 m.
• Number of rounds = 2,000 m ÷ 2,000 m = 1 round.

2. (b) 400-metre Stadium Track:
• Number of rounds = 2,000 m ÷ 400 m = 5 rounds.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Square field perimeter (2,000 m) = 1 round 1.5 M

✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Circle Properties:
• (a) Diameter: Diameter = 2 × Radius = 2 × 35 cm = 70 cm.
• (b) Relationship: The diameter is always twice the length of the radius (D = 2r), passing straight through the circle's center.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Diameter = 70 cm 1 M
Formula D = 2r stated 1 M
Q12 Raju has ₹100. He spends 1/2 of his money on a book, and 1/4 of his total money on a pen.
(a) How much does the book cost?
(b) How much does the pen cost?
(c) How much money does Raju have left? Halves and Quarters • Fraction Sharing 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Expenditure:
• (a) Book (1/2): ₹100 ÷ 2 = ₹50.
• (b) Pen (1/4): ₹100 ÷ 4 = ₹25.
• (c) Money Left: ₹100 - (₹50 + ₹25) = ₹100 - ₹75 = ₹25.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Book = ₹50 1 M
Pen = ₹25 1 M
Remaining = ₹25 1 M
Q13 In a secret code, each letter is replaced by its position in the alphabet (A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26).
Decode the secret message: '13 - 1 - 20 - 8 - 19'. What word does it spell? Play with Patterns • Secret Message Cipher 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Decoding Cipher:
• 13 = M
• 1 = A
• 20 = T
• 8 = H
• 19 = S
The secret message spells MATHS.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct decoding to spell MATHS 2 M
Q14 A florist has 58 roses. She wants to make bouquets containing 6 roses each.
(a) How many complete bouquets can she make?
(b) How many roses will be left over? Tables and Shares • Division with Remainder 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Division:
• 58 ÷ 6 = 9 with a remainder of 4.
• (a) Complete bouquets: 9 bouquets (9 × 6 = 54 roses).
• (b) Leftover roses: 58 - 54 = 4 roses left over.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
9 bouquets 1 M
4 leftover roses 1 M
Q15 State True or False:

(b) 1 kilometre equals 100 metres.
(c) Half past 4 in the afternoon is written as 16:30 in 24-hour time. Review Questions • True or False 3 Marks

Answers:

• (b) False: 1 km = 1000 metres.
• (c) True: 4:30 PM = 12:00 + 4:30 = 16:30 hours.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
True, False, True with explanations 3 M
🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Daily Walking Distance Goal

Real-World Math Context: Doctor advises grandfather to walk 2 kilometres every day to stay fit. Grandfather walks around a square park that measures 125 metres on each side.

Analytical Task: How many complete rounds of the park must grandfather take to achieve his 2 km goal?

Step 1: Perimeter of square park:
Perimeter = 4 × 125 m = 500 metres per round.
Step 2: Rounds for 2 km:
2 km = 2,000 metres.
Rounds required = 2,000 m ÷ 500 m = 4 rounds.
Grandfather must walk 4 complete rounds.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for park perimeter 500 m; 1 Mark for 4 rounds.

Chapter 3: A Trip to Bhopal

Excursion Planning, Bus Seating, Petrol Pumps, Narmada Bridge & Bhimbetka Time Math

📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Total students going on the Bhopal trip from Class 1 to Class 5 are: Class 1: 33, Class 2: 32, Class 3: 42, Class 4: 50, Class 5: 53. (a) How many total children are going on the trip? (b) If the school hires 4 buses with 50 seats each, how many children will get seats? (c) How many children will be left without seats? Excursion Bus Seating Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Total Children Going on the Trip:
   33 (Class 1)
+ 32 (Class 2)
+ 42 (Class 3)
+ 50 (Class 4)
+ 53 (Class 5)
-------
210 total children.

(b) Seating Capacity of 4 Buses:
• Each bus has 50 seats.
• 4 buses have 4 × 50 = 200 seats.
So, 200 children will get seats.

(c) Children Left Without Seats:
• 210 - 200 = 10 children will be left without seats.
(They will share seats with classmates).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct sum of 210 children 1.0 M
Correct calculation of 200 seats in 4 buses 1.0 M
Difference of 10 children without seats 1.0 M
Q2 At the Bhopal lake, ticket prices are: Double-Decker Boat: ₹30 (45 mins), Motor Boat: ₹25 (20 mins), Paddle Boat: ₹15 (30 mins), Oar Boat: ₹15 (45 mins). (a) Indra and Bhanu first took the motor boat and then the oar boat. How much total did they pay for both? (b) Which boat makes two trips in 1 hour? Boat Ride Rates & Time Budgeting 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Cost for Indra and Bhanu:
• Motor boat: Ticket = ₹25 per person. For 2 persons = 2 × ₹25 = ₹50.
• Oar boat: Ticket = ₹15 per person. For 2 persons = 2 × ₹15 = ₹30.
• Total amount paid = ₹50 + ₹30 = ₹80.

(b) Boat that makes 2 trips in 1 hour:
• 1 hour = 60 minutes.
• Time for 1 trip must be 60 ÷ 2 = 30 minutes.
• Looking at the table, the Paddle Boat has a trip duration of 30 minutes. Hence, the paddle boat makes two trips in 1 hour.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct total fare of ₹80 for 2 children 1.5 M
Correct identification of Paddle Boat with 30 min duration 1.5 M
Q3 At the Bhopal lake, a motorboat charges ₹25 per person for a 20-minute ride, and a paddle boat charges ₹15 per person for a 45-minute ride. A group of 4 children have ₹100 and 40 minutes time. Which boat ride can they take? Travel Budget • Boating Rates 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Comparison of Boating Options for 4 Children:
Available Budget = ₹100, Available Time = 40 minutes.

1. Motorboat Option:
• Total Cost = 4 × ₹25 = ₹100 (Within budget).
• Duration = 20 minutes (Well within 40 minutes).

2. Paddle Boat Option:
• Total Cost = 4 × ₹15 = ₹60 (Within budget).
• Duration = 45 minutes (Exceeds available 40 minutes).

Conclusion: They can only take the motorboat ride because the paddle boat exceeds their 40-minute time limit.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Budget calculation for both boats (₹100 vs ₹60) 1.5 M
Time limit comparison selecting motorboat (20 mins vs 45 mins) 1.5 M
Q4 At a highway petrol pump, the driver filled 65 litres of diesel into a bus tank. If the diesel price was ₹84 per litre, calculate the total amount the school paid for the diesel. Fuel Arithmetic • Filling Bus Tanks 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Multiplication Calculation:
    6 5 litres
  ×  8 4 rupees
-----------
    2 6 0   (65 × 4)
+ 5 2 0 0   (65 × 80)
-----------
  5 4 6 0

The school paid ₹5,460 for the diesel.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct partial products (260 and 5200) 1.0 M
Final total bill of ₹5,460 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.
(a) What is the shape of a brick?
(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M
Q6 During a school trip to Bhopal, 4 buses stop at a petrol pump to fill diesel. Each bus takes 50 litres of diesel. If 1 litre of diesel costs ₹70:
(a) How many litres of diesel are filled in total?
(b) What is the total bill amount the school must pay? A Trip to Bhopal • Diesel Fuel Budget 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
• (a) Total diesel: 4 buses × 50 L = 200 litres.
• (b) Total bill amount: 200 L × ₹70/L = ₹14,000.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
200 litres 1.5 M
₹14,000 bill 1.5 M

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Petrol Pump Refueling Time

Real-World Math Context: Two buses stop at a petrol pump to refill diesel. Each bus needs 50 litres of diesel. 1 litre of diesel costs ₹45. The pump fills diesel at a rate of 10 litres per minute.

Analytical Task: What is the total bill for both buses, and how long does it take to fill both buses?

Step 1: Total diesel required:
2 buses × 50 litres = 100 litres.
Step 2: Total cost:
100 litres × ₹45/litre = ₹4,500.
Step 3: Total filling time:
100 litres ÷ 10 litres/min = 10 minutes.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for cost ₹4,500; 1 Mark for time 10 minutes.

Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 4: Tick-Tick-Tick

Clock Reading, Movement of Minute Hand, 12h vs 24h Formats & Expiry Date Verification

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Understanding clock mechanics: the minute hand moves 1 small mark (6°) in 1 minute, 5 marks (30°) from one number to the next.
  • Calculating the time taken by the minute hand to move from one digit to another.
  • Differentiating morning (am) and afternoon/evening (pm).
  • Reading manufacturing (MFG) and expiry dates on ghee tins, medicines, and butter packets.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 How many minutes does it take the minute hand to move from: (a) 2 to 7, (b) 12 to 8, (c) 1 to 4, (d) 7 to 10? Minute Hand Movement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Each number on the clock represents 5 minutes.
• (a) From 2 to 7: (7 - 2) = 5 numbers. 5 × 5 = 25 minutes.
• (b) From 12 to 8: 8 numbers forward. 8 × 5 = 40 minutes.
• (c) From 1 to 4: (4 - 1) = 3 numbers. 3 × 5 = 15 minutes.
• (d) From 7 to 10: (10 - 7) = 3 numbers. 3 × 5 = 15 minutes.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct answers for (a) and (b) 1.5 M
Correct answers for (c) and (d) 1.5 M
Q2 Amrit bought butter on 15 May 2026. It was written on the packet 'Best before 180 days from the date of packing'. The date of packing was 15/01/2026. Should Amrit eat this butter or not? Explain your calculation. Product Expiry Date Check 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step 1: Calculate days from 15 January 2026 to 15 May 2026:
• January: 31 - 15 = 16 days remaining.
• February 2026 (non-leap year) = 28 days.
• March = 31 days.
• April = 30 days.
• May (up to 15th) = 15 days.
• Total days passed = 16 + 28 + 31 + 30 + 15 = 120 days.

Step 2: Compare with expiry limit:
• The packet is safe for 180 days.
• Since 120 days < 180 days, the butter has NOT expired.
• Conclusion: Yes, Amrit can safely eat the butter.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Accurate monthly day count sum = 120 days 2.0 M
Valid comparison and safe consumption verdict 1.0 M
Q3 Munia was born on 5th March 2025. She cut her first tooth on 15th September 2025 and started walking on 2nd February 2026. (a) How old was she in completed months when she cut her first tooth? (b) How many months after birth did she start walking? Developmental Timeline • Munia's Milestones 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Timeline Milestones:
Date of Birth: 5th March 2025

1. (a) First Tooth (15th September 2025):
From March to September = 6 months (March 5 to Sept 5 = 6 months) + 10 days.
Munia was 6 months and 10 days old.

2. (b) Walking (2nd February 2026):
From March 2025 to January 2026 = 10 months. Adding up to Feb 2 = nearly 11 months old.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
First tooth age: 6 months 10 days 1.5 M
Walking milestone age: approx 11 months 1.5 M
Q4 Is the year 2028 a leap year? How many days will the months of February and the entire year 2028 have? Calendar Math • Number of Days in a Year 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Leap Year Test:
A year is a leap year if its last two digits are divisible by 4.
• 28 ÷ 4 = 7 (completely divisible, remainder 0).
Therefore, 2028 is a leap year.

Days Count:
• February 2028 will have 29 days.
• The entire year 2028 will have 366 days (instead of the normal 365 days).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Divisibility rule proving 2028 is a leap year 1.0 M
February has 29 days and total year has 366 days 1.0 M

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Missing a Train Due to 24-Hour Time Format

Real-World Math Context: Ranjit has a train ticket with departure time printed as '05:30'. He reaches the railway station at 5:00 in the evening on the same day.

Analytical Task: Did Ranjit catch his train or miss it? Explain why the railway ticket printed 05:30.

Ranjit missed his train.
Explanation: Indian Railways uses the 24-hour clock. '05:30' represents 5:30 am in the early morning. If the train were at 5:30 in the evening, the ticket would have printed '17:30'. Since Ranjit arrived at 5:00 pm (17:00), his train had already departed 11 hours and 30 minutes earlier.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for missed train conclusion; 1 Mark for explaining 05:30 am vs 17:30 pm.

Chapter 5: The Way The World Looks

Gappu's Balloon Journey, Aerial Maps, Matching Top Views & Perspective Drawing

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Perspective changes with distance: objects look big and wide at the near end, but small and narrow at the distant end.
  • Gappu's view from a gas balloon: seeing the top of the bed, fan blades, chairs, and park benches.
  • Matching top view (bird's-eye view) with ground view for bowls, shoes, cars, and railway lines.
  • Navigating classroom floor maps and finding doors, windows, and blackboards.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Why do the two sides of a railway track or road look wide and far apart near your feet, but appear to meet at a single point in the far distance? Perspective Illusion Explanation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Scientific & Geometric Reason:
This phenomenon is called Perspective Convergence.
• Objects or distances that are close to our eyes subtend a larger visual angle, so they appear wider and larger.
• As objects move farther away, their visual angle shrinks, so they appear smaller and narrower.
• Although the two rails of a train track are strictly parallel and always equal distance apart on the ground, our eye's perspective causes them to appear to converge toward a 'vanishing point' on the horizon.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Mentions perspective / visual angle effect 1.5 M
Explains parallel lines appearing to converge at horizon 1.5 M
Q2 Draw or describe: (a) The top view of a teacup, (b) The side view of a pressure cooker, (c) The top view of a railway track. Perspective Views • Everyday Objects 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Perspective Projections:
• (a) Top View of a Teacup: Shows two concentric circles (the inner circle representing the cup rim and the outer circle representing the saucer) with a small oval loop on one side representing the handle.
• (b) Side View of a Pressure Cooker: Shows the cylindrical metallic body, the side handle, the lid clamp, and the safety weight (whistle) sitting on the top vent pipe.
• (c) Top View of a Railway Track: Shows two parallel vertical lines with horizontal wooden or concrete sleepers spaced evenly across them like a ladder.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Accurate geometric description of teacup top view 1.0 M
Accurate side view of cooker 1.0 M
Accurate top view of railway track 1.0 M
Q3 On a neighbourhood map, Neha starts at the Park facing East. She walks 50 metres straight, turns 90° right, walks 30 metres, and enters the Library. (a) In which direction did she walk after turning? (b) In which direction is the Library from the Park? Grid Map Navigation • Directions 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Spatial Navigation:
1. Direction after turning:
Starting facing East, turning 90° to the right means she is now facing South. She walked 30 metres South.

2. Position of Library relative to Park:
The Library is 50 m East and 30 m South of the Park. Therefore, the Library is located to the South-East of the Park.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Right turn from East = South 1.5 M
Combined position = South-East 1.5 M
Q4 A boy looks at a dice. The sum of opposite faces of a standard dice is always 7. If the top face shows 3 and the front face shows 2, what numbers are on: (a) the bottom face, (b) the back face? Isometric 3D Blocks • Visual Reasoning 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Opposite Face Rule (Sum = 7):
• (a) Bottom Face: Opposite to the top face (3).
Bottom = 7 - 3 = 4.

• (b) Back Face: Opposite to the front face (2).
Back = 7 - 2 = 5.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Bottom face = 4 1.0 M
Back face = 5 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.
(a) What is the shape of a brick?
(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M
Q6 During a school trip to Bhopal, 4 buses stop at a petrol pump to fill diesel. Each bus takes 50 litres of diesel. If 1 litre of diesel costs ₹70:
(a) How many litres of diesel are filled in total?
(b) What is the total bill amount the school must pay? A Trip to Bhopal • Diesel Fuel Budget 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
• (a) Total diesel: 4 buses × 50 L = 200 litres.
• (b) Total bill amount: 200 L × ₹70/L = ₹14,000.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
200 litres 1.5 M
₹14,000 bill 1.5 M
Q7 The world record for Men's High Jump is 245 cm and the Long Jump world record is 895 cm.
(a) Express the High Jump record in metres and centimetres.
(b) How many more centimetres are needed for the high jump record to reach exactly 2.5 metres (250 cm)? Long and Short • Height Comparisons 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Calculations:
• (a) High Jump: 245 cm = 2 metres 45 centimetres.
• (b) Difference: 250 cm - 245 cm = 5 centimetres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
2 m 45 cm 1 M
5 cm difference 1 M
Q8 A packet of butter shows 'Manufactured: 15/01/26' and 'Best before 180 days from manufacture'. Can it be consumed safely on 15th April 2026? Explain. Tick-Tick-Tick • Expiry Dates on Food 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Date Verification:
• From 15th Jan to 15th April is exactly 3 months (approx. 90 days).
• Since 90 days is well within the 180-day shelf life, the butter is completely safe to consume on 15th April 2026.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Time elapsed is ~90 days 1 M
Safe to consume within 180 days 1 M
Q9 Kiran took a loan of ₹300 from a bank to set up her scrap shop. She pays back ₹51 every month for six months.
(a) How much money does she pay back in total?
(b) How much extra money (interest) did she pay to the bank? The Junk Seller • Loan and Interest 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Loan Repayment:
• (a) Total paid: 6 months × ₹51/month = ₹306.
• (b) Extra money (interest): ₹306 - ₹300 = ₹6.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Total repayment = ₹306 1.5 M
Interest = ₹6 1.5 M
Q10 A doctor prescribes a cough syrup bottle of 150 mL for a child. The child takes a 5 mL dose three times a day.
(a) How much cough syrup does the child take in one day?
(b) For how many days will the 150 mL bottle last? Jugs and Mugs • Measuring Medicine Dose 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Dosage Calculations:
• (a) Daily intake: 3 doses × 5 mL = 15 mL per day.
• (b) Days bottle lasts: 150 mL ÷ 15 mL/day = 10 days.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
15 mL daily 1.5 M
Bottle lasts 10 days 1.5 M
Q11 A cyclist has a bicycle wheel with a radius of 35 cm.
(a) What is the diameter of the wheel?
(b) What is the relationship between the radius and diameter of any circle? Carts and Wheels • Circle Geometry 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Circle Properties:
• (a) Diameter: Diameter = 2 × Radius = 2 × 35 cm = 70 cm.
• (b) Relationship: The diameter is always twice the length of the radius (D = 2r), passing straight through the circle's center.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Diameter = 70 cm 1 M
Formula D = 2r stated 1 M
Q12 Raju has ₹100. He spends 1/2 of his money on a book, and 1/4 of his total money on a pen.
(a) How much does the book cost?
(b) How much does the pen cost?
(c) How much money does Raju have left? Halves and Quarters • Fraction Sharing 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Expenditure:
• (a) Book (1/2): ₹100 ÷ 2 = ₹50.
• (b) Pen (1/4): ₹100 ÷ 4 = ₹25.
• (c) Money Left: ₹100 - (₹50 + ₹25) = ₹100 - ₹75 = ₹25.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Book = ₹50 1 M
Pen = ₹25 1 M
Remaining = ₹25 1 M
Q13 In a secret code, each letter is replaced by its position in the alphabet (A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26).
Decode the secret message: '13 - 1 - 20 - 8 - 19'. What word does it spell? Play with Patterns • Secret Message Cipher 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Decoding Cipher:
• 13 = M
• 1 = A
• 20 = T
• 8 = H
• 19 = S
The secret message spells MATHS.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct decoding to spell MATHS 2 M
Q14 A florist has 58 roses. She wants to make bouquets containing 6 roses each.
(a) How many complete bouquets can she make?
(b) How many roses will be left over? Tables and Shares • Division with Remainder 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Division:
• 58 ÷ 6 = 9 with a remainder of 4.
• (a) Complete bouquets: 9 bouquets (9 × 6 = 54 roses).
• (b) Leftover roses: 58 - 54 = 4 roses left over.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
9 bouquets 1 M
4 leftover roses 1 M
Q15 State True or False:
(a) A square has 4 equal sides and its perimeter is 4 × side.
(b) 1 kilometre equals 100 metres.
(c) Half past 4 in the afternoon is written as 16:30 in 24-hour time. Review Questions • True or False 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Answers:
• (a) True: Perimeter = 4 × side.
• (b) False: 1 km = 1000 metres.
• (c) True: 4:30 PM = 12:00 + 4:30 = 16:30 hours.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
True, False, True with explanations 3 M
🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Classroom Blackboard Location Map

Real-World Math Context: In a classroom top-view plan, the teacher's table is at (3, 5), the entrance door is at (1, 1), and student benches are arranged in 4 columns from X = 2 to 5.

Analytical Task: If a student enters through the door and walks straight North along the left aisle, which direction should she turn to face the teacher's table?

She should walk North up to Y = 5, and then turn Right (East) to face the teacher's table at (3, 5).
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for North direction; 1 Mark for Right / East turn.

Chapter 6: The Junk Seller

Kiran's Junk Shop, Rate Lists, Loan Interest, Calculating Earnings & Column Multiplication

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Real-life entrepreneurship: Kiran's story of establishing a junk shop in Patna.
  • Understanding bank loans vs. moneylenders: calculating simple interest and monthly installments.
  • Rate lists for scrap materials: waste paper, newspaper, iron, brass, plastic.
  • Multiplication of currency and mental math strategies (e.g., 9 × 12 = 9 × 10 + 9 × 2).
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Hari and Babu each wanted to buy a handcart worth ₹300. (a) Hari took a loan of ₹300 from a bank. He pays back ₹51 every month for 6 months. How much does he pay back in total? (b) Babu took a loan of ₹300 from Chuni Lal (moneylender). After 6 months, he pays back ₹360. Who has to pay back more money, and by how much? Loan Comparison: Bank vs. Moneylender 4 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Hari's Bank Loan Repayment:
• Monthly installment = ₹51.
• Duration = 6 months.
• Total paid = 6 × ₹51 = ₹306.
(Extra interest paid to bank = ₹306 - ₹300 = ₹6).

(b) Babu's Moneylender Loan Repayment:
• Total paid to Chuni Lal = ₹360.
(Extra interest paid to moneylender = ₹360 - ₹300 = ₹60).

Comparison:
• Babu pays back more money.
• Difference = ₹360 - ₹306 = ₹54 more.
This demonstrates why institutional bank loans are far fairer than unregulated moneylenders.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Hari's total repayment ₹306 1.5 M
Babu's total repayment ₹360 1.0 M
Comparison showing Babu pays ₹54 more 1.5 M
Q2 Using Kiran's rate list (Newspaper: ₹5/kg, Iron: ₹12/kg, Brass: ₹180/kg, Plastic: ₹10/kg), calculate the total amount Kiran pays for: (a) 42 kg of newspaper, (b) 25 kg of iron, (c) 3 kg of brass. Scrap Rate Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) 42 kg of Newspaper at ₹5/kg:
• 42 × 5 = 42 × (10 ÷ 2) = 420 ÷ 2 = ₹210.

(b) 25 kg of Iron at ₹12/kg:
• 25 × 12 = 25 × (10 + 2) = 250 + 50 = ₹300.

(c) 3 kg of Brass at ₹180/kg:
• 3 × 180 = ₹540.

Total amount Kiran pays:
Total = ₹210 + ₹300 + ₹540 = ₹1,050.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct calculation for all three items 2.0 M
Total bill sum ₹1,050 1.0 M
Q3 Kiran the junk seller buys scrap from collectors at these rates:
• Newspaper: ₹14/kg
• Iron: ₹22/kg
• Brass: ₹280/kg
Calculate the total amount Kiran pays for 40 kg newspaper, 15 kg iron, and 2 kg brass. Commercial Math • Kiran's Rate List 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Bill Calculation:
1. Newspaper (40 kg): 40 × ₹14 = ₹560.
2. Iron (15 kg): 15 × ₹22 = ₹330.
3. Brass (2 kg): 2 × ₹280 = ₹560.

Total Amount Paid:
₹560 + ₹330 + ₹560 = ₹1,450.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Newspaper (₹560) and Iron (₹330) 1.0 M
Brass (₹560) 1.0 M
Total bill = ₹1,450 1.0 M
Q4 Hariyal took a loan of ₹300 from a bank and repaid ₹340 after 6 months. Babu took a loan of ₹300 from a moneylender and paid ₹60 every month for 6 months. Who paid back more money, and by how much? Loan & Interest • Financial Comparison 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Comparison of Repayments:
• Hariyal (Bank): Total paid back = ₹340.

• Babu (Moneylender):
Paid ₹60 every month for 6 months = 6 × ₹60 = ₹360.

Difference:
₹360 - ₹340 = ₹20.

Babu paid back ₹20 more than Hariyal because of the moneylender's higher interest rate.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Babu's total payment calculation (6 × 60 = 360) 1.5 M
Correct comparison: Babu paid ₹20 more 1.5 M

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Kiran's Reselling Profit Margin

Real-World Math Context: Kiran buys 152 kg of plastic from scrap collectors at ₹10 per kg. She transports it to a big recycling factory and sells it at ₹12 per kg.

Analytical Task: How much money does Kiran earn as profit on this plastic sale?

Step 1: Profit per kg:
Selling Price - Cost Price = ₹12 - ₹10 = ₹2 per kg.
Step 2: Total profit on 152 kg:
152 kg × ₹2/kg = ₹304 profit.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for ₹2/kg margin; 1 Mark for ₹304 total profit.

Chapter 7: Jugs and Mugs

Bunny and Banno's Wedding Feast, Capacity in Litres & Millilitres & Medical Doses

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Bunny and Banno celebrate their wedding anniversary: animals drink kheer in millilitres and litres.
  • Capacity conversion: 1 Litre (l) = 1,000 Millilitres (ml).
  • Combinations that make 1 Litre: 500 ml + 500 ml; 250 ml × 4; 200 ml × 5; 100 ml × 10.
  • Estimating liquid usage: water used in bathing, cooking, washing clothes, and eye-drop droppers.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 At the wedding party: (a) 1,000 ants arrive. Each ant drinks only 1 ml of kheer. How much kheer do all 1,000 ants drink together? (b) A donkey wonders how 1,000 ants can drink kheer. 10 frogs also arrive, and each frog drinks 100 ml. How much kheer do 10 frogs drink? Bunny and Banno's Wedding Feast Arithmetic 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Kheer for 1,000 ants:
• Each ant drinks 1 ml.
• Total kheer = 1,000 × 1 ml = 1,000 ml = 1 Litre.

(b) Kheer for 10 frogs:
• Each frog drinks 100 ml.
• Total kheer = 10 × 100 ml = 1,000 ml = 1 Litre.

Interesting observation: Both the 1,000 tiny ants and the 10 frogs drink the exact same total amount of kheer (1 Litre each!).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct calculation for ants (1,000 ml = 1 l) 1.5 M
Correct calculation for frogs (1,000 ml = 1 l) 1.5 M
Q2 Look at the table of water used by a family of 4 people in one day: Cooking and drinking: 30 l, Washing clothes: 40 l, Cleaning pots and pans: 20 l, Bathing: 75 l. (a) What is the total water used by the family in one day? (b) How much water will they use in 1 week (7 days)? Household Water Usage & Conservation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Total water used in 1 day:
   30 l (cooking/drinking)
+ 40 l (washing clothes)
+ 20 l (cleaning pans)
+ 75 l (bathing)
-------
165 Litres per day.

(b) Water used in 1 week (7 days):
• 165 l × 7 days = 1,155 Litres.
(Working: 100 × 7 = 700; 60 × 7 = 420; 5 × 7 = 35; 700 + 420 + 35 = 1,155 l).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct sum of 165 Litres for 1 day 1.5 M
Accurate multiplication 165 × 7 = 1,155 Litres 1.5 M
Q3 At Bunny's wedding feast, 1,000 ants arrived to drink kheer. Each ant drinks only 1 mL of kheer. (a) How many millilitres of kheer do 1,000 ants drink in total? (b) Convert this quantity into litres. Capacity Multiples • Bunny's Wedding Feast 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Calculation:
1. Total Millilitres:
1,000 ants × 1 mL = 1,000 mL.

2. Convert to Litres:
Since 1,000 mL = 1 Litre,
1,000 mL ÷ 1,000 = 1 Litre of kheer in total.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Multiplication (1,000 mL) 1.0 M
Conversion to 1 Litre 1.0 M
Q4 A family uses 6 buckets of water for bathing (each bucket holds 15 L), 3 buckets for washing clothes (each holds 20 L), and 45 L for cooking and drinking. What is the family's total daily water consumption? Water Measurement • Daily Household Usage 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Total Usage:
1. Bathing: 6 buckets × 15 L = 90 L.
2. Washing Clothes: 3 buckets × 20 L = 60 L.
3. Cooking & Drinking: = 45 L.

Total Daily Consumption:
90 L + 60 L + 45 L = 195 Litres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Bathing (90 L) and clothes (60 L) calculated 1.5 M
Grand total sum of 195 Litres 1.5 M

Q6 During a school trip to Bhopal, 4 buses stop at a petrol pump to fill diesel. Each bus takes 50 litres of diesel. If 1 litre of diesel costs ₹70:
(a) How many litres of diesel are filled in total?
(b) What is the total bill amount the school must pay? A Trip to Bhopal • Diesel Fuel Budget 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
• (a) Total diesel: 4 buses × 50 L = 200 litres.
• (b) Total bill amount: 200 L × ₹70/L = ₹14,000.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
200 litres 1.5 M
₹14,000 bill 1.5 M

Q9 Kiran took a loan of ₹300 from a bank to set up her scrap shop. She pays back ₹51 every month for six months.
(a) How much money does she pay back in total?
(b) How much extra money (interest) did she pay to the bank? The Junk Seller • Loan and Interest 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Loan Repayment:
• (a) Total paid: 6 months × ₹51/month = ₹306.
• (b) Extra money (interest): ₹306 - ₹300 = ₹6.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Total repayment = ₹306 1.5 M
Interest = ₹6 1.5 M
Q10 A doctor prescribes a cough syrup bottle of 150 mL for a child. The child takes a 5 mL dose three times a day.
(a) How much cough syrup does the child take in one day?
(b) For how many days will the 150 mL bottle last? Jugs and Mugs • Measuring Medicine Dose 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Dosage Calculations:
• (a) Daily intake: 3 doses × 5 mL = 15 mL per day.
• (b) Days bottle lasts: 150 mL ÷ 15 mL/day = 10 days.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
15 mL daily 1.5 M
Bottle lasts 10 days 1.5 M
Q11 A cyclist has a bicycle wheel with a radius of 35 cm.
(a) What is the diameter of the wheel?
(b) What is the relationship between the radius and diameter of any circle? Carts and Wheels • Circle Geometry 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Circle Properties:
• (a) Diameter: Diameter = 2 × Radius = 2 × 35 cm = 70 cm.
• (b) Relationship: The diameter is always twice the length of the radius (D = 2r), passing straight through the circle's center.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Diameter = 70 cm 1 M
Formula D = 2r stated 1 M
Q12 Raju has ₹100. He spends 1/2 of his money on a book, and 1/4 of his total money on a pen.
(a) How much does the book cost?
(b) How much does the pen cost?
(c) How much money does Raju have left? Halves and Quarters • Fraction Sharing 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Expenditure:
• (a) Book (1/2): ₹100 ÷ 2 = ₹50.
• (b) Pen (1/4): ₹100 ÷ 4 = ₹25.
• (c) Money Left: ₹100 - (₹50 + ₹25) = ₹100 - ₹75 = ₹25.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Book = ₹50 1 M
Pen = ₹25 1 M
Remaining = ₹25 1 M
Q13 In a secret code, each letter is replaced by its position in the alphabet (A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26).
Decode the secret message: '13 - 1 - 20 - 8 - 19'. What word does it spell? Play with Patterns • Secret Message Cipher 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Decoding Cipher:
• 13 = M
• 1 = A
• 20 = T
• 8 = H
• 19 = S
The secret message spells MATHS.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct decoding to spell MATHS 2 M
Q14 A florist has 58 roses. She wants to make bouquets containing 6 roses each.
(a) How many complete bouquets can she make?
(b) How many roses will be left over? Tables and Shares • Division with Remainder 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Division:
• 58 ÷ 6 = 9 with a remainder of 4.
• (a) Complete bouquets: 9 bouquets (9 × 6 = 54 roses).
• (b) Leftover roses: 58 - 54 = 4 roses left over.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
9 bouquets 1 M
4 leftover roses 1 M
Q15 State True or False:
(a) A square has 4 equal sides and its perimeter is 4 × side.
(b) 1 kilometre equals 100 metres.
(c) Half past 4 in the afternoon is written as 16:30 in 24-hour time. Review Questions • True or False 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Answers:
• (a) True: Perimeter = 4 × side.
• (b) False: 1 km = 1000 metres.
• (c) True: 4:30 PM = 12:00 + 4:30 = 16:30 hours.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
True, False, True with explanations 3 M
🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Hospital Intravenous (IV) Saline Bottle Rate

Real-World Math Context: A hospital saline bottle holds 500 ml of fluid. A nurse sets the drip so that the patient receives 2 ml of saline every minute.

Analytical Task: How many minutes will it take for the entire 500 ml bottle to finish? Express your answer in hours and minutes.

Step 1: Calculate total minutes:
500 ml ÷ 2 ml/min = 250 minutes.
Step 2: Convert into hours and minutes:
250 ÷ 60 = 4 with a remainder of 10.
So, 250 minutes = 4 hours and 10 minutes.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for 250 minutes; 1 Mark for 4 hours 10 minutes.
8
Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 8: Carts and Wheels

Circles, Centre, Radius, Diameter, Compass Drawing & Spinning Tops (Charkhi)

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Identifying round circular objects: bullock cart wheels, bicycle wheels, bangles, coins, chapati.
  • Key circle vocabulary: Centre, Radius (r), Diameter (d), and Circumference.
  • Relationship between radius and diameter: Diameter = 2 × Radius; Radius = Diameter ÷ 2.
  • Technique for drawing a circle using a nail and string, or a geometric compass.
  • Making spinning tops (charkhi) and balancing the stick at the exact centre.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Fill in the blanks: (a) If the radius of a circle is 4 cm, its diameter is __ cm. (b) If the diameter of a wheel is 18 cm, its radius is __ cm. (c) Which circle is larger: Circle A with radius 6 cm or Circle B with diameter 10 cm? Radius and Diameter Relationships 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Formula: Diameter = 2 × Radius
• Diameter = 2 × 4 cm = 8 cm.

(b) Formula: Radius = Diameter ÷ 2
• Radius = 18 cm ÷ 2 = 9 cm.

(c) Comparison between Circle A and Circle B:
• Circle A radius = 6 cm (so Diameter = 2 × 6 = 12 cm).
• Circle B diameter = 10 cm (so Radius = 10 ÷ 2 = 5 cm).
• Since 6 cm > 5 cm, Circle A is larger.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct answers for (a) and (b) 1.5 M
Accurate comparison showing Circle A is larger 1.5 M
Q2 Zakir, Appu, Naina, and Guddo made cardboard spinning tops. Zakir put his stick near the edge, Appu put it halfway between center and edge, Naina put it slightly off-center, while Guddo put her stick at the exact geometric centre. (a) Whose top will not spin at all? (b) Whose top will spin the best? Explain why. Spinning Top Physics & Circle Centre 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Top that will not spin at all:
Zakir's top will not spin at all (or will wobble and instantly fall over) because placing the stick at the outer edge creates an extreme weight imbalance.

(b) Top that will spin the best:
Guddo's top will spin the best.
• Reason: When the pivot stick is placed at the exact center of the circle, the radius is equal in all directions. This distributes mass uniformly and balances the gravitational forces evenly around the axis of rotation, allowing the top to spin smoothly and stably for a long time.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Identifies Zakir's top as failing 1.0 M
Identifies Guddo's top as best with center balance explanation 2.0 M
Q3 If the radius of a bicycle wheel is 35 cm, what is its diameter? If the diameter of a giant Ferris wheel is 24 metres, what is its radius? Circle Geometry • Radius & Diameter 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Formula: Diameter = 2 × Radius  |  Radius = Diameter ÷ 2

1. Bicycle Wheel:
Radius = 35 cm
Diameter = 2 × 35 cm = 70 cm.

2. Giant Ferris Wheel:
Diameter = 24 m
Radius = 24 m ÷ 2 = 12 metres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Bicycle diameter: 70 cm 1.0 M
Ferris wheel radius: 12 m 1.0 M
Q4 Describe how to draw a circle of radius 4 cm using a ruler and a geometric compass. What is the role of the fixed sharp point? Compass Construction • Drawing Circles 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Steps to Draw a Circle:
1. Setting the Radius: Open the arms of the compass. Place the sharp metal pointer at 0 cm mark on the ruler and expand the pencil tip to exactly the 4 cm mark.
2. Marking the Centre: Press the sharp metal pointer firmly onto paper without shifting; this point is the Centre (O) of the circle.
3. Drawing: Holding the compass strictly by the top handle, rotate the pencil 360° smoothly around the centre to trace the circular boundary.
The fixed sharp point ensures that every point on the circumference remains at an exact constant distance of 4 cm from the centre.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Setting 4 cm measurement on ruler 1.0 M
Center anchor and 360° pencil rotation 1.0 M
Explaining constant distance from center 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.
(a) What is the shape of a brick?
(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M
Q6 During a school trip to Bhopal, 4 buses stop at a petrol pump to fill diesel. Each bus takes 50 litres of diesel. If 1 litre of diesel costs ₹70:
(a) How many litres of diesel are filled in total?
(b) What is the total bill amount the school must pay? A Trip to Bhopal • Diesel Fuel Budget 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
• (a) Total diesel: 4 buses × 50 L = 200 litres.
• (b) Total bill amount: 200 L × ₹70/L = ₹14,000.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
200 litres 1.5 M
₹14,000 bill 1.5 M

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Playground Circle Marker with String

Real-World Math Context: The sports teacher needs to draw a circular kabaddi ring on the ground with a diameter of 8 metres. She has a wooden peg and a coil of rope.

Analytical Task: How long should she cut the rope to tie between the central peg and the chalk stick?

Answer: 4 metres.
Explanation: The length of the rope between the central fixed peg (centre) and the rotating chalk stick represents the radius of the circle.
Radius = Diameter ÷ 2 = 8 m ÷ 2 = 4 metres.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for identifying rope as radius; 1 Mark for calculating 4 metres.
9
Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 9: Halves and Quarters

Fractions: 1/2, 1/4, 3/4, Fraction Walls, Splitting Objects & Metric Fractions

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Concept of a fraction: equal parts of a whole.
  • Half (1/2), One-quarter (1/4), and Three-quarters (3/4).
  • Two quarters make one half: 1/4 + 1/4 = 2/4 = 1/2.
  • Four quarters make one whole: 4/4 = 1.
  • Fractions of collections (e.g., half of 20 apples = 10; quarter of ₹100 = ₹25).
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Calculate: (a) 1/2 of ₹100, (b) 1/4 of a metre (in cm), (c) 3/4 of a kilogram (in grams), (d) 1/2 of a litre (in ml). Fraction of Amounts & Metric Conversions 4 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) 1/2 of ₹100:
• ₹100 ÷ 2 = ₹50.

(b) 1/4 of a metre:
• 1 m = 100 cm.
• 100 cm ÷ 4 = 25 cm.

(c) 3/4 of a kilogram:
• 1 kg = 1,000 g.
• 1/4 of 1,000 g = 250 g.
• 3/4 = 3 × 250 g = 750 grams.

(d) 1/2 of a litre:
• 1 l = 1,000 ml.
• 1,000 ml ÷ 2 = 500 ml.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct values for (a) and (b) 2.0 M
Correct values for (c) and (d) 2.0 M
Q2 Rani had a chocolate bar with 12 equal pieces. She gave 1/4 of it to Reena, 1/3 of it to Sugatha, and ate the rest herself. How many pieces did each girl get? Word Problem on Splitting Food 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Total pieces in chocolate bar = 12.

• Reena's share: 1/4 of 12 = 12 ÷ 4 = 3 pieces.
• Sugatha's share: 1/3 of 12 = 12 ÷ 3 = 4 pieces.
• Pieces given away: 3 + 4 = 7 pieces.
• Rani's share: 12 - 7 = 5 pieces.

Check: 3 + 4 + 5 = 12 pieces. Correct!
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Reena = 3 pieces; Sugatha = 4 pieces 2.0 M
Rani = 5 pieces with verification 1.0 M
Q3 Look at the vegetable price list:
• Tomato: ₹16 per kg
• Potato: ₹20 per kg
• Carrot: ₹32 per kg
Calculate the total cost of 1/2 kg tomatoes, 3/4 kg potatoes, and 1 1/4 kg carrots. Fractional Pricing • Vegetable Market 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Pricing:
1. Cost of 1/2 kg Tomatoes: 1/2 × ₹16 = ₹8.
2. Cost of 3/4 kg Potatoes: 3/4 × ₹20 = 3 × (20 ÷ 4) = 3 × ₹5 = ₹15.
3. Cost of 1 1/4 kg Carrots:
• 1 kg = ₹32
• 1/4 kg = ₹32 ÷ 4 = ₹8
• Total = ₹32 + ₹8 = ₹40.

Grand Total Bill:
₹8 + ₹15 + ₹40 = ₹63.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Tomatoes (₹8) and Potatoes (₹15) 1.0 M
Carrots (₹40) 1.0 M
Total bill = ₹63 1.0 M
Q4 A square sheet of paper is folded in half diagonally, and then folded in half again. (a) What fraction of the original sheet is each small triangle? (b) How many such small triangles make three-quarters (3/4) of the sheet? Fraction Arithmetic • Cutting Shapes 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Folding:
1. First diagonal fold divides the square into 2 equal halves (1/2 each).
2. Second fold divides the halves into 4 equal triangles (1/4 each).

• (a) Fraction of each small triangle: 1/4 of the original square sheet.
• (b) To make 3/4: Exactly 3 small triangles make 3/4 of the sheet (3 × 1/4 = 3/4).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Part (a): 1/4 fraction 1.0 M
Part (b): 3 small triangles 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.

(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks

Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M

Decoding Cipher:
• 13 = M
• 1 = A
• 20 = T
• 8 = H
• 19 = S
The secret message spells MATHS.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct decoding to spell MATHS 2 M

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Vegetable Price List Fraction Math

Real-World Math Context: At the sabzi mandi: Tomato costs ₹16 per kg, Potato costs ₹20 per kg, and Onion costs ₹24 per kg. Mrs. Sharma buys 1/2 kg tomatoes, 3/4 kg potatoes, and 1/4 kg onions.

Analytical Task: How much total money does Mrs. Sharma spend?

Cost breakdown:
• Tomatoes: 1/2 × ₹16 = ₹8.
• Potatoes: 3/4 × ₹20 = 3 × ₹5 = ₹15.
• Onions: 1/4 × ₹24 = ₹6.
• Total spent = ₹8 + ₹15 + ₹6 = ₹29.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1.5 Marks for itemized fractions; 1.5 Marks for total ₹29.
