Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 10: Play with Patterns

Tiling Patterns, Number Towers, Magic Squares & Secret Message Decryption

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Tiling patterns (tessellations) on floors and historical monuments.
  • Number towers: each box is the sum of the two boxes directly below it.
  • Magic Squares (3×3 grid): where every row, column, and diagonal adds up to the exact same magic sum (e.g., 15 or 45).
  • Secret message decoding using alphabet-number substitutions.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 In a number tower, the bottom row has the numbers 20, 30, and 50. Fill in the missing numbers for the middle row and the top box. State the rule used. Number Tower Solving Rule 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Rule of the Number Tower:
The number in each higher box is the sum of the two boxes directly below it.

• Middle row left box: 20 + 30 = 50.
• Middle row right box: 30 + 50 = 80.
• Top box: Sum of middle row = 50 + 80 = 130.

Completed Tower:
    [ 130 ]
  [ 50 ] [ 80 ]
[ 20 ] [ 30 ] [ 50 ]
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
States addition rule of number tower 1.0 M
Correct values: middle row 50, 80; top box 130 2.0 M
Q2 Complete a 3×3 magic square using numbers from 1 to 9 so that every row, column, and diagonal adds up to 15. Magic Square Construction 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
The standard 3×3 magic square with sum 15 is:
816
357
492
• Rows: 8+1+6 = 15; 3+5+7 = 15; 4+9+2 = 15.
• Columns: 8+3+4 = 15; 1+5+9 = 15; 6+7+2 = 15.
• Diagonals: 8+5+2 = 15; 6+5+4 = 15. All equal 15!
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct 3x3 grid layout with numbers 1 to 9 2.0 M
Verification of rows, columns, and diagonals = 15 1.0 M
Q3 In a 3×3 magic square using numbers 1 to 9, the sum of numbers along every row, column, and diagonal is 15. If the central cell is 5, explain why 5 must be in the centre. Magic Squares • 3x3 Grid Puzzle 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Magic Square Mathematical Logic:
1. Sum of all numbers 1 to 9:
1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 6 + 7 + 8 + 9 = 45.
Dividing by 3 rows gives the magic constant: 45 ÷ 3 = 15.

2. Why 5 must be in the Centre:
The central cell belongs to 4 lines (1 horizontal row, 1 vertical column, and 2 diagonals). Therefore, it must be the exact median (average) of all 9 numbers. Pairing numbers that sum to 10 (1+9=10, 2+8=10, 3+7=10, 4+6=10) with 5 in the centre always yields: 10 + 5 = 15.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Calculating total sum 45 and magic constant 15 1.5 M
Explaining central cell median alignment with 4 intersecting lines 1.5 M
Q4 In a number tower, the bottom row has numbers 10, 20, 30. The rule is that adding two adjacent lower numbers gives the number above them. Find the top number of the tower. Number Pyramids • Addition Rules 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Tower Addition Steps:
• Bottom Row (Row 1): [ 10 ]    [ 20 ]    [ 30 ]

• Middle Row (Row 2):
Left box = 10 + 20 = 30.
Right box = 20 + 30 = 50.
Row 2: [ 30 ]    [ 50 ]

• Top Row (Row 3):
Top box = 30 + 50 = 80.

The number in the top box is 80.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Middle row calculation (30 and 50) 1.0 M
Top number = 80 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.
(a) What is the shape of a brick?
(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M
Q6 During a school trip to Bhopal, 4 buses stop at a petrol pump to fill diesel. Each bus takes 50 litres of diesel. If 1 litre of diesel costs ₹70:

Step-by-Step Solution:

• (b) Total bill amount: 200 L × ₹70/L = ₹14,000.

Q11 A cyclist has a bicycle wheel with a radius of 35 cm.

• (a) Diameter: Diameter = 2 × Radius = 2 × 35 cm = 70 cm.
• (b) Relationship: The diameter is always twice the length of the radius (D = 2r), passing straight through the circle's center.

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Coded Message for Class Picnic

Real-World Math Context: Teenu writes a secret note using numbers for letters: A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26. Her note reads: '13-5-5-20   13-5   1-20   14-15-15-14'.

Analytical Task: Decrypt Teenu's secret note.

Decoded Note: 'MEET ME AT NOON'.
• 13=M, 5=E, 5=E, 20=T (MEET)
• 13=M, 5=E (ME)
• 1=A, 20=T (AT)
• 14=N, 15=O, 15=O, 14=N (NOON)
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for letter substitution method; 1 Mark for 'MEET ME AT NOON'.

Chapter 11: Tables and Shares

Counting Legs of Animals, Division as Equal Shelving & Group Distribution

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Multiplying by grouping: counting legs of cats (4 legs each), spiders (8 legs each), and frogs.
  • Arranging pots, almirah shelves, and plants in arrays (rows × columns).
  • Division strategies: jumping backwards on a number line and repeated subtraction.
  • Distributing items among people with equal shares and determining leftovers.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 (a) Gayatri saw 28 cat legs peeping out from under a wooden cupboard. How many cats were there inside? (b) Some ducks and cows are standing in a field. Together they have 20 legs. If there are 3 cows, how many ducks are there? Leg Counting & Grouping 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Cats under the cupboard:
• 1 cat has 4 legs.
• Number of cats = Total legs ÷ 4
• Number of cats = 28 ÷ 4 = 7 cats.

(b) Ducks and Cows leg puzzle:
• 1 cow has 4 legs. 3 cows have 3 × 4 = 12 legs.
• Remaining legs = Total legs - Cow legs = 20 - 12 = 8 legs.
• 1 duck has 2 legs.
• Number of ducks = 8 ÷ 2 = 4 ducks.
Check: (3 cows × 4 legs) + (4 ducks × 2 legs) = 12 + 8 = 20 legs. Correct!
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct calculation for cats (7 cats) 1.5 M
Correct two-step deduction for ducks (4 ducks) 1.5 M
Q2 Shyama wants to plant 18 rose saplings in rectangular flowerbeds. In how many different rectangular array arrangements (rows × plants in each row) can she plant them? List all pairs. Array Multiplication • Shyama's Garden 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Factor Pairs of 18:
We find all pairs of whole numbers whose product is 18:
1. 1 row of 18 plants: 1 × 18 = 18
2. 2 rows of 9 plants each: 2 × 9 = 18
3. 3 rows of 6 plants each: 3 × 6 = 18
4. 6 rows of 3 plants each: 6 × 3 = 18
5. 9 rows of 2 plants each: 9 × 2 = 18
6. 18 rows of 1 plant each: 18 × 1 = 18

She can arrange them in 6 different rectangular arrays.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Listing factor pairs of 18 1.5 M
Stating all distinct row-column configurations 1.5 M
Q3 Explain how you can easily construct the multiplication table of 7 using the tables of 2 and 5. Distributive Property • Splitting Tables 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Using the Distributive Property: 7 = 2 + 5
To find any multiple of 7, add the corresponding multiple of 2 and multiple of 5:
• 7 × 1 = (2 × 1) + (5 × 1) = 2 + 5 = 7
• 7 × 2 = (2 × 2) + (5 × 2) = 4 + 10 = 14
• 7 × 3 = (2 × 3) + (5 × 3) = 6 + 15 = 21
• 7 × 4 = (2 × 4) + (5 × 4) = 8 + 20 = 28
• 7 × 8 = (2 × 8) + (5 × 8) = 16 + 40 = 56
• 7 × 10 = (2 × 10) + (5 × 10) = 20 + 50 = 70.
This demonstrates that large multiplication tables can be constructed by combining smaller, easy tables.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Stating 7 = 2 + 5 principle 1.5 M
Demonstrating at least 3 correct row additions 1.5 M
Q4 A carpenter has 84 wooden books. If he puts 7 books on each shelf, how many shelves will be completely filled? Division as Grouping • Shelving Books 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Division:
84 ÷ 7
• 70 ÷ 7 = 10
• 14 ÷ 7 = 2
• 10 + 2 = 12

Exactly 12 shelves will be completely filled.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct division yielding 12 shelves 2.0 M

Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 12: How Heavy? How Light?

Pan Balance Weighing, Grams & Kilograms, Postal Tariffs & Weight Estimation

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Comparing weights using a beam balance scale.
  • 1 Kilogram (kg) = 1,000 Grams (g); 1/2 kg = 500 g; 1/4 kg = 250 g.
  • Indian Post Office parcel and letter tariff rates based on weight increments.
  • Estimating weights of heavy objects (elephant, tractor, sack of wheat) vs light objects (pencil, feather).
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Look at the postal rates: A parcel weighing 50 grams or less costs ₹5. For every additional 50 grams or part thereof, it costs ₹3. Radha wants to send a book parcel weighing 225 grams to her friend. How much postal stamp value must she affix on the parcel? Postal Tariff Calculation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step 1: Break down the weight of 225 grams into 50 g slabs:
• First 50 grams = 50 g (remaining = 225 - 50 = 175 g).
• Next 50 grams = 50 g (remaining = 125 g).
• Next 50 grams = 50 g (remaining = 75 g).
• Next 50 grams = 50 g (remaining = 25 g).
• Final 25 grams counts as an additional 50 g slab.
Total slabs = 1 basic slab + 4 additional slabs.

Step 2: Calculate cost:
• First 50 g = ₹5.
• 4 additional slabs of 50 g = 4 × ₹3 = ₹12.
• Total postal stamp value = ₹5 + ₹12 = ₹17.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct breakdown of 225 g into 5 slabs 1.5 M
Accurate calculation giving ₹17 stamp cost 1.5 M
Q2 Look at the postal rates:
• Single letter (up to 20 g): ₹5.00
• For every additional 20 g: ₹2.00
Calculate the postage stamps required to send a heavy official letter weighing 75 grams. Postal Mathematics • Calculating Postage 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Postal Calculation:
Total weight = 75 grams.

1. First 20 grams: Cost = ₹5.00.
2. Remaining weight: 75 g - 20 g = 55 grams.
• Next 20 g (21–40 g): + ₹2.00
• Next 20 g (41–60 g): + ₹2.00
• Remaining 15 g (part of next 20 g, 61–75 g): + ₹2.00
Total additional 20 g units = 3 units.
Additional cost = 3 × ₹2.00 = ₹6.00.

Total Postage Required:
₹5.00 + ₹6.00 = ₹11.00.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Base charge for first 20 g (₹5.00) 1.0 M
Identifying 3 additional units (₹6.00) 1.0 M
Total postage: ₹11.00 1.0 M
Q3 State whether the following objects should be measured in 'grams' or 'kilograms':
(a) A tube of toothpaste
(b) A sack of rice
(c) A golden ring
(d) A newborn baby Weight Estimation • Grams vs. Kilograms 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Units of Weight:
• (a) A tube of toothpaste: Grams (g) (approx. 100 g - 150 g)
• (b) A sack of rice: Kilograms (kg) (approx. 25 kg - 50 kg)
• (c) A golden ring: Grams (g) (approx. 4 g - 8 g)
• (d) A newborn baby: Kilograms (kg) (approx. 2.5 kg - 3.5 kg).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
All four units correctly identified 2.0 M
Q4 How many 250-gram packets of tea make 1 1/2 kilograms? Weight Conversion • Balancing Scales 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Calculation:
1. Convert 1 1/2 kg into grams:
1 kg = 1,000 g
1/2 kg = 500 g
Total weight = 1,000 g + 500 g = 1,500 grams.

2. Number of 250 g packets:
1,500 ÷ 250 = 6 packets.

Exactly 6 packets of tea make 1 1/2 kg.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Conversion to 1,500 g 1.0 M
Division: 1500 ÷ 250 = 6 packets 1.0 M
Q5 A standard brick has length 20 cm, breadth 10 cm, and height 7 cm.
(a) What is the shape of a brick?
(b) What is the area of its largest face?
(c) How many bricks of 20 cm length placed end-to-end make a line 1 metre (100 cm) long? Building with Bricks • Brick Geometry & Measurement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Brick Mathematics:
• (a) Shape: A standard brick is a cuboid.
• (b) Area of largest face: Length × Breadth = 20 cm × 10 cm = 200 square centimetres (cm²).
• (c) Number of bricks in 1 m: 1 m = 100 cm. Number of bricks = 100 ÷ 20 = 5 bricks.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Cuboid shape 1 M
Area = 200 cm² 1 M
5 bricks 1 M
Q6 During a school trip to Bhopal, 4 buses stop at a petrol pump to fill diesel. Each bus takes 50 litres of diesel. If 1 litre of diesel costs ₹70:

(b) What is the total bill amount the school must pay? A Trip to Bhopal • Diesel Fuel Budget 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
• (a) Total diesel: 4 buses × 50 L = 200 litres.
• (b) Total bill amount: 200 L × ₹70/L = ₹14,000.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
200 litres 1.5 M
₹14,000 bill 1.5 M

Q9 Kiran took a loan of ₹300 from a bank to set up her scrap shop. She pays back ₹51 every month for six months.

(b) How much extra money (interest) did she pay to the bank? The Junk Seller • Loan and Interest 3 Marks

• (b) Extra money (interest): ₹306 - ₹300 = ₹6.

Q10 A doctor prescribes a cough syrup bottle of 150 mL for a child. The child takes a 5 mL dose three times a day.
(a) How much cough syrup does the child take in one day?
(b) For how many days will the 150 mL bottle last? Jugs and Mugs • Measuring Medicine Dose 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Dosage Calculations:
• (a) Daily intake: 3 doses × 5 mL = 15 mL per day.
• (b) Days bottle lasts: 150 mL ÷ 15 mL/day = 10 days.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
15 mL daily 1.5 M
Bottle lasts 10 days 1.5 M
Q11 A cyclist has a bicycle wheel with a radius of 35 cm.
(a) What is the diameter of the wheel?
(b) What is the relationship between the radius and diameter of any circle? Carts and Wheels • Circle Geometry 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Circle Properties:
• (a) Diameter: Diameter = 2 × Radius = 2 × 35 cm = 70 cm.
• (b) Relationship: The diameter is always twice the length of the radius (D = 2r), passing straight through the circle's center.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Diameter = 70 cm 1 M
Formula D = 2r stated 1 M
Q12 Raju has ₹100. He spends 1/2 of his money on a book, and 1/4 of his total money on a pen.
(a) How much does the book cost?
(b) How much does the pen cost?
(c) How much money does Raju have left? Halves and Quarters • Fraction Sharing 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Expenditure:
• (a) Book (1/2): ₹100 ÷ 2 = ₹50.
• (b) Pen (1/4): ₹100 ÷ 4 = ₹25.
• (c) Money Left: ₹100 - (₹50 + ₹25) = ₹100 - ₹75 = ₹25.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Book = ₹50 1 M
Pen = ₹25 1 M
Remaining = ₹25 1 M
Q13 In a secret code, each letter is replaced by its position in the alphabet (A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26).
Decode the secret message: '13 - 1 - 20 - 8 - 19'. What word does it spell? Play with Patterns • Secret Message Cipher 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Decoding Cipher:
• 13 = M
• 1 = A
• 20 = T
• 8 = H
• 19 = S
The secret message spells MATHS.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct decoding to spell MATHS 2 M
Q14 A florist has 58 roses. She wants to make bouquets containing 6 roses each.
(a) How many complete bouquets can she make?
(b) How many roses will be left over? Tables and Shares • Division with Remainder 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Division:
• 58 ÷ 6 = 9 with a remainder of 4.
• (a) Complete bouquets: 9 bouquets (9 × 6 = 54 roses).
• (b) Leftover roses: 58 - 54 = 4 roses left over.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
9 bouquets 1 M
4 leftover roses 1 M
Q15 State True or False:
(a) A square has 4 equal sides and its perimeter is 4 × side.
(b) 1 kilometre equals 100 metres.
(c) Half past 4 in the afternoon is written as 16:30 in 24-hour time. Review Questions • True or False 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Answers:
• (a) True: Perimeter = 4 × side.
• (b) False: 1 km = 1000 metres.
• (c) True: 4:30 PM = 12:00 + 4:30 = 16:30 hours.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
True, False, True with explanations 3 M
🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Balancing Scales with Broken Stone Weights

Real-World Math Context: A vendor had a single 4 kg stone weight. It broke into two pieces of 1 kg and 3 kg.

Analytical Task: How can the vendor weigh out exactly 2 kg of firewood using these two pieces on a two-pan balance?

Procedure:
• Place the 3 kg piece on the left pan.
• Place the 1 kg piece on the right pan.
• Add firewood to the right pan until both pans balance in equilibrium.
• Since Left Pan (3 kg) = Right Pan (1 kg + Firewood), the weight of firewood is 3 kg - 1 kg = 2 kg.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1.5 Marks for correct pan placement; 1.5 Marks for mathematical balance proof.

Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 13: Fields and Fences

Perimeter of Irregular Polygons, Wire Fencing Calculations & Grid Square Area Counting

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Perimeter is the total continuous length of the outer boundary of a closed planar shape.
  • Calculating perimeter by adding lengths of all straight boundary edges.
  • Fencing fields with wire: single round vs. multiple rounds (e.g., 3-wire barbed fence).
  • Counting whole and half squares on a square centimetre grid to estimate surface area.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 Rahmat is a farmer. He has a field with sides measuring 9 m, 15 m, 21 m, and 9 m. (a) What is the length of the boundary of Rahmat's field? (b) Rahmat bought a roll of 70 metres of barbed wire to fence his field. How much wire will be left over after putting one complete round of fence? Boundary Length & Wire Requirement 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Length of boundary (Perimeter):
Sum of all sides = 9 m + 15 m + 21 m + 9 m = 54 metres.

(b) Wire remaining:
• Total wire in roll = 70 metres.
• Wire used for 1 round = 54 metres.
• Wire left over = 70 m - 54 m = 16 metres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct boundary sum 54 metres 1.5 M
Correct remaining wire 16 metres 1.5 M
Q2 A square has a side of 6 cm. (a) What is its perimeter? (b) If four 1 cm squares are cut out from its four corners, what is the perimeter of the new shape? Explain why the perimeter changes or stays the same. Square Grid Area & Perimeter 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
(a) Perimeter of original square:
Perimeter = 4 × side = 4 × 6 cm = 24 cm.

(b) Perimeter after cutting 1 cm squares from each corner:
• When a 1 cm square is removed from a corner, the two outer 1 cm edges that are removed are replaced by two inner 1 cm edges that form the new stepped corner.
• Therefore, the total boundary length remains exactly 24 cm!
• Conclusion: The perimeter stays the same (24 cm), even though the area has decreased by 4 sq cm.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Original perimeter 24 cm 1.0 M
Explains corner cut effect and confirms perimeter remains 24 cm 2.0 M
Q3 A shape is drawn on a 1 cm square grid. It covers 12 complete squares and 6 half squares. (a) What is the area of this shape in square centimetres? (b) If each square has side 1 cm, what is the area of 1 unit square? Area & Perimeter • Grid Squares 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Grid Area Calculation:
• Area of 1 unit square with side 1 cm = 1 cm × 1 cm = 1 sq cm.

1. 12 complete squares = 12 × 1 sq cm = 12 sq cm.
2. 6 half squares = 6 × (1/2 sq cm) = 3 sq cm.

Total Area of the Shape:
12 + 3 = 15 sq cm.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Area of 1 unit square = 1 sq cm 1.0 M
Combining complete and half squares accurately (12 + 3) 1.0 M
Total area: 15 sq cm 1.0 M
Q4 Ganpat has a field with sides 15 m, 20 m, 18 m, and 27 m. He bought a 100-metre roll of barbed wire to fence the boundary once. (a) What is the perimeter of his field? (b) How much wire will be left over after fencing? Field Fencing • Barbed Wire Math 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Solution:
1. Perimeter of the Field:
Perimeter = Sum of all 4 sides
= 15 m + 20 m + 18 m + 27 m = 80 metres.

2. Wire Left Over:
Wire roll length = 100 m
Wire used = 80 m
Leftover wire = 100 m - 80 m = 20 metres.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct sum of sides = 80 m 1.5 M
Subtraction 100 - 80 = 20 m left 1.5 M

🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Three-Strand Wire Fence Budget

Real-World Math Context: A farmer wants to put a 3-strand barbed wire fence around a rectangular field that is 40 metres long and 25 metres wide. Fencing wire costs ₹15 per metre.

Analytical Task: Calculate the total length of wire needed and the total cost.

Step 1: Perimeter:
2 × (40 + 25) = 2 × 65 = 130 metres.
Step 2: 3 strands:
130 m × 3 = 390 metres of wire.
Step 3: Total cost:
390 m × ₹15/m = ₹5,850.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1 Mark for perimeter (130 m); 1 Mark for 3 strands (390 m); 1 Mark for ₹5,850.

Classic Track: Math-Magic • Class 4 Mathematics (Classic CBSE)

Chapter 14: Smart Charts

Chapati Charts (Pie Charts), Road Traffic Tally Marks, Family Trees & Attendance Charts

Chapter Highlights, Mathematical Ideas & Key Properties
  • Using tally marks for traffic flow observations: bicycles, cars, auto-rickshaws, buses, trucks.
  • Chapati charts (circle graphs / pie charts): interpreting 1/2, 1/4, and 3/4 fractional shares.
  • Reading family generation charts and student school absence records.
  • Drawing conclusions and converting data tables into visual bar graphs.
📖 NCERT Textbook Exercise Solutions & Step-by-Step Working (15 Solved Questions)
Q1 In a class of 36 students, a Chapati Chart shows their favorite seasons: 1/2 of the students like Winter, 1/4 like Rainy season, and 1/4 like Summer. (a) How many students like Winter? (b) How many students like Summer? (c) How many more students like Winter than Summer? Chapati Chart Interpretation 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Total students in class = 36.

• (a) Students who like Winter:
1/2 of 36 = 36 ÷ 2 = 18 students.

• (b) Students who like Summer:
1/4 of 36 = 36 ÷ 4 = 9 students.

• (c) Difference:
18 - 9 = 9 more students like Winter than Summer.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Winter = 18 students 1.0 M
Summer = 9 students 1.0 M
Difference = 9 students 1.0 M
Q2 Look at the primary school attendance record for a rainy day:
• Class 1: Total 30, Absent 4
• Class 2: Total 28, Absent 3
• Class 3: Total 32, Absent 5
• Class 4: Total 35, Absent 2
• Class 5: Total 25, Absent 1
(a) What was the total number of students enrolled in the school? (b) How many total students were present on that day? Smart Charts • Attendance Analysis 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Step-by-Step Data Analysis:
1. (a) Total Students Enrolled:
30 + 28 + 32 + 35 + 25 = 150 students.

2. Total Students Absent:
4 + 3 + 5 + 2 + 1 = 15 students.

3. (b) Total Students Present:
150 (Total) - 15 (Absent) = 135 students.

In total, 135 students were present on that rainy day.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Total enrolled = 150 1.0 M
Total absent = 15 1.0 M
Total present = 135 1.0 M
Q3 A bar graph shows favourite fruits of Class 4 children. The bar for Mango has height 18 units, Apple 12 units, Banana 8 units, and Orange 6 units. (a) Which fruit is most popular? (b) How many more children prefer Mango over Banana? Data Visualization • Bar Charts 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Bar Graph Reading:
• Mango = 18
• Apple = 12
• Banana = 8
• Orange = 6

1. (a) Most Popular Fruit: Mango (tallest bar with 18 votes).
2. (b) Difference between Mango and Banana:
18 - 8 = 10 more children.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Most popular fruit identified as Mango 1.0 M
Correct difference: 18 - 8 = 10 1.0 M
Q4 A student counts vehicles passing her gate in 15 minutes: 14 bicycles, 9 cars, and 6 auto-rickshaws. Represent each vehicle count using 5-bar tally marks. Tally Tables • Traffic Survey 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Tally Marks Representation:
• Bicycles (14): ||||   ||||   |||| (Two groups of five + four)
• Cars (9): ||||   |||| (One group of five + four)
• Auto-rickshaws (6): ||||   | (One group of five + one).
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Accurate 5-bar grouping for 14, 9, and 6 2.0 M

Diameter = 70 cm 1 M
Formula D = 2r stated 1 M
Q12 Raju has ₹100. He spends 1/2 of his money on a book, and 1/4 of his total money on a pen.
(a) How much does the book cost?
(b) How much does the pen cost?
(c) How much money does Raju have left? Halves and Quarters • Fraction Sharing 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Fractional Expenditure:
• (a) Book (1/2): ₹100 ÷ 2 = ₹50.
• (b) Pen (1/4): ₹100 ÷ 4 = ₹25.
• (c) Money Left: ₹100 - (₹50 + ₹25) = ₹100 - ₹75 = ₹25.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Book = ₹50 1 M
Pen = ₹25 1 M
Remaining = ₹25 1 M
Q13 In a secret code, each letter is replaced by its position in the alphabet (A=1, B=2, C=3, ... Z=26).
Decode the secret message: '13 - 1 - 20 - 8 - 19'. What word does it spell? Play with Patterns • Secret Message Cipher 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Decoding Cipher:
• 13 = M
• 1 = A
• 20 = T
• 8 = H
• 19 = S
The secret message spells MATHS.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
Correct decoding to spell MATHS 2 M
Q14 A florist has 58 roses. She wants to make bouquets containing 6 roses each.
(a) How many complete bouquets can she make?
(b) How many roses will be left over? Tables and Shares • Division with Remainder 2 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Division:
• 58 ÷ 6 = 9 with a remainder of 4.
• (a) Complete bouquets: 9 bouquets (9 × 6 = 54 roses).
• (b) Leftover roses: 58 - 54 = 4 roses left over.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
9 bouquets 1 M
4 leftover roses 1 M
Q15 State True or False:
(a) A square has 4 equal sides and its perimeter is 4 × side.
(b) 1 kilometre equals 100 metres.
(c) Half past 4 in the afternoon is written as 16:30 in 24-hour time. Review Questions • True or False 3 Marks
✅ Step-by-Step Model Answer & Solution:
Answers:
• (a) True: Perimeter = 4 × side.
• (b) False: 1 km = 1000 metres.
• (c) True: 4:30 PM = 12:00 + 4:30 = 16:30 hours.
CBSE Step-by-Step Marking Scheme
True, False, True with explanations 3 M
🌟 NEP 2020 Competency-Based Case Study (CBQ)

Road Safety Tally Observation

Real-World Math Context: Anita stood near a junction for 30 minutes and counted vehicles using tally marks: Cycle-rickshaws: '卌 卌 卌', Bicycles: '卌 卌 卌 卌 卌', Buses: '卌 ||', Cars: '卌 卌 ||'.

Analytical Task: Which vehicle was seen the most, and what is the total count of all vehicles observed by Anita?

Counts:
• Cycle-rickshaws: 15
• Bicycles: 25
• Buses: 7
• Cars: 12
• Most seen vehicle: Bicycles (25).
• Total vehicles: 15 + 25 + 7 + 12 = 59 vehicles.
CBSE Assessment Rubric: 1.5 Marks for vehicle breakdown and most frequent; 1.5 Marks for total 59.
